THE METRICS ^qBUuZOnP

Cricket x Data · Lesson 02 ^S9KFN1jH

Data: Cricsheet (CC BY 4.0) ^xUkUiNt5

13/37 ^e5WLwlJK

Brier = average of (forecast − what happened)² ^4TZcwtDU

0 is perfect.  Always saying 50% scores 0.25.  Lower is better. ^6t0NqDc6

Rule: ^UExp6biO

(1−1)² + (0−0)² + (1−0)² + (0−0)²   =  1   ÷ 4  =  0.25 ^pZBIxGE7

Model: ^TcfBfq7N

(.85−1)² + (.10−0)² + (.40−0)² + (.25−0)²   =  0.26  ÷ 4  =  0.06 ^UdIdGFJS

One confident miss (chase C) costs the rule the maximum penalty of 1. ^fPo1XorS

A rule that's right 75% of the time scores no better than a coin flip. ^I8bCkZu7

Certainty is expensive. ^Bf17V7Qh

Metric 3: AUC, the ranking test ^7HSw0mBU

BUILD IT ^RniJLPkM

Cricket x Data · Lesson 02 ^0ny5698t

Data: Cricsheet (CC BY 4.0) ^KBkwr1cq

26/37 ^NWiTOoVG

SCOREBOARD = ["runs_needed", "balls_left", "wickets_left"]

model = LogisticRegression().fit(train[SCOREBOARD], train.chasing_won)
p     = model.predict_proba(test[SCOREBOARD])[:, 1] ^B8W7dT9S

fit = choose the βs on 17,499 past overs ^KlGhaoNZ

predict_proba = a probability for 3,877 overs it has never seen ^YoqRPtIy

[:, 1] = "chance the chasing side wins" (column 0 is the chance it loses). ^9Ur2Y6iu

   "text": "2023 brought the Impact Player rule (deeper batting, so a wicket costs less).\nIt fits the pattern, but we haven't tested it. \u2192 distribution shift",
